<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the permanence conjecture for finite weakly reversible mass-action systems with fixed positive reaction rates. Every positive stoichiometric compatibility class admits one compact convex forward-invariant set that every positive trajectory in that class enters in finite time, even when the class is unbounded. The set depends only on the network, the rates, and the class; the entry time may depend on the initial state. Thus, after its entry time, every concentration satisfies the same positive lower and finite upper bounds throughout that class.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $C\subset\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^d$ be finite, let $R\subset C\times C$ be weakly reversible, and fix positive rate constants in <a href="#eq:mass-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mass-action">[eq:mass-action]</a>. For every positive stoichiometric class $P$ there is a nonempty compact convex set $K_P\subset P$ with the following properties. Every initial point $x^0\in P$ gives a unique global positive solution, and there is a finite $T(x^0)\geq0$ such that $$x(t)\in K_P\qquad(t\geq T(x^0)).$$ Moreover, $K_P$ is forward invariant. In particular, there is $\varepsilon_P\in(0,1)$, depending only on the network, the fixed rates, and $P$, such that every such solution satisfies $$\label{eq:uniform-bounds} \varepsilon_P\leq x_i(t)\leq\varepsilon_P^{-1} \qquad(1\leq i\leq d,\ t\geq T(x^0)).$$
  - Explanation: Classwise permanence gives global positive solutions and a common compact convex absorbing set; the entry time may depend on the initial state.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine)): Let $m\geq1$ be an integer, let $a<b$ be real numbers, and let $E:[a,b]^m\to(0,\infty)$ be any function. There is a finite nonempty family $\Lambda$ of labels with $$r_\lambda\in[a,b]^m, \qquad b_\lambda(h)=o(h^a)\quad(h\to0+),$$ such that the following implication holds. If $h_\nu\to0+$, $p^\nu\in\mathbb R^m$ converges to $p\in[a,b]^m$, and a fixed label $\lambda\in\Lambda$ is active at $X_{h_\nu}(p^\nu)$ for every $\nu$, then $$\left\\|p-r_\lambda\right\\|_\infty<E(r_\lambda).$$ The slopes and the family are independent of $h$. No regularity is assumed of $E$, and the approximating vectors $p^\nu$ need not lie in $[a,b]^m$.
  - Explanation: A finite affine family approximates limiting exponent vectors at active labels with an arbitrary positive tolerance, including discontinuous tolerances.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-activity)): Let $m\geq1$, $a<b$, and $E:[a,b]^m\to(0,\infty)$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:affine">2</a>. Fix a family $\Lambda$ supplied by that lemma. There is $h_0\in(0,1)$ such that, for every $0<h<h_0$ and every $p\in[a,b]^m$, each label $\lambda\in\Lambda$ active at $X_h(p)$ satisfies $$\left\\|p-r_\lambda\right\\|_\infty<E(r_\lambda).$$
  - Explanation: After the family is fixed, the activity approximation holds uniformly on the exponent cube at sufficiently small scales.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-plateau)): For $0<h\leq h_P$, let $$M_h=F_h(c), \qquad K_h=\{x\in\mathbb R^d:F_h(x)=M_h\}.$$ There is a constant label with value $M_h$, and $F_h\leq M_h$ on $\mathbb R^d$. The set $K_h$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, and $$\label{eq:plateau-containment} K_h\subset\operatorname{int}B_{\sqrt h}.$$
  - Explanation: A constant active label makes the maximum plateau compact and convex and places it strictly inside the next scale box.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-increase)): Fix $0<h\leq h_P$. For every $x\in B_h$ and every $\lambda\in A_h(x)$, $$\label{eq:active-flux-sign} r_\lambda\cdot f(x)\geq0, \qquad r_\lambda\cdot f(x)>0\quad\text{if }r_\lambda\notin S^\perp.$$ Moreover, there is a number $\delta_h>0$ such that $$\label{eq:uniform-active-increase} r_\lambda\cdot f(x)\geq\delta_h \quad\text{whenever }x\in B_h,\ \lambda\in A_h(x), \ r_\lambda\notin S^\perp.$$ At every point of $P\setminus K_h$, all active slopes lie outside $S^\perp$. In particular, <a href="#eq:uniform-active-increase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-active-increase">[eq:uniform-active-increase]</a> applies to every active label at each $x\in B_h\cap(P\setminus K_h)$.
  - Explanation: Weak reversibility and monomial ordering give nonnegative active directional derivatives and a uniform positive derivative for slopes outside the stoichiometric orthogonal complement.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trapping)): Fix $0<h\leq h_P$. Along any solution segment contained in $B_h$, the function $t\mapsto F_h(x(t))$ is nondecreasing. If the segment $x([s,t])$ is contained in $B_h\cap(P\setminus K_h)$, then $$\label{eq:trajectory-increase} F_h(x(t))-F_h(x(s))\geq\delta_h(t-s).$$ Every solution starting in $K_h$ exists for all nonnegative time and remains in $K_h$. In particular, solutions starting in $K_h\cap P$ remain in $K_h\cap P$. Finally, any global solution in $P$ that stays in $B_h$ from some time onward enters $K_h$ in finite time and then remains there.
  - Explanation: The affine minimum is nondecreasing in the scale box and increases uniformly outside the plateau in the fixed class; this yields invariance and finite entry.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Complexes and reactions** ([source](#eq:mass-action)): A finite reaction network consists of a set of *complexes* $C\subset\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^d$ and a set of directed *reactions* $R\subset C\times C$.
- **Weak reversibility** ([source](#eq:mass-action)): The network is *weakly reversible* if for every $y\to y'\in R$ there is a directed path from $y'$ to $y$.
- **Linkage class** ([source](#eq:mass-action)): A *linkage class* is a connected component of the underlying undirected graph.
- **Mass-action vector field** ([source](#eq:mass-action)): The mass-action equation is $$\label{eq:mass-action} \dot x=f(x):=\sum_{y\to y'\in R} k_{y\to y'}x^y(y'-y), \qquad x^y:=\prod_{i=1}^d x_i^{y_i}.$$
- **Stoichiometric subspace and positive class** ([source](#eq:mass-action)): The *stoichiometric subspace* and the *positive stoichiometric class* of $c\in\mathbb R_{>0}^d$ are $$S:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{y'-y:y\to y'\in R\}, \qquad P:=(c+S)\cap\mathbb R_{>0}^d.$$
- **Persistence** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A global positive trajectory is *persistent* if $\liminf_{t\to\infty}x_i(t)>0$ for every $i$.
- **Permanence on a class** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We call the system *permanent on $P$* if every solution starting in $P$ is global and positive, and there is one compact set $K\subset P$ that eventually contains each such solution.
- **Complex balance** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A system is *complex-balanced* if it has a positive state at which the total incoming and outgoing reaction fluxes agree at each complex.
- **Exponential coordinate map** ([source](#sec:affine)): For $m\geq1$, $u\in\mathbb R^m$, and $0<h<1$, put $$X_h(u)=(h^{u_1},\ldots,h^{u_m}).$$
- **Affine label** ([source](#sec:affine)): A *label* $\lambda=(r_\lambda,b_\lambda)$ consists of a slope $r_\lambda\in\mathbb R^m$, independent of $h$, and an offset $b_\lambda(h)$ defined for all sufficiently small positive $h$.
- **Finite affine minimum** ([source](#sec:affine)): For a finite nonempty family $\Lambda$ of labels, write $$F_h(x)=\min_{\lambda\in\Lambda} \bigl(r_\lambda\cdot x+b_\lambda(h)\bigr).$$
- **Active label** ([source](#sec:affine)): A label is *active at $x$* if its affine value equals $F_h(x)$.
- **Scale box** ([source](#eq:network-activity)): Use $X_h$ from Section <a href="#sec:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine">2</a> with $m=d$, and for $0<h<1$ put $$B_h=[h,h^{-1}]^d.$$
- **Plateau** ([source](#stmt-prop-plateau)): For $0<h\leq h_P$, let $$M_h=F_h(c), \qquad K_h=\{x\in\mathbb R^d:F_h(x)=M_h\}.$$
- **Net upward cut flux** ([source](#eq:monomial-order)): For $1\leq j<m$, define the net upward flux across the cut at $a_j$ by $$\begin{aligned} Q_j(x)={}& \sum_{\substack{u\to u'\in R\,:\\r\cdot u\leq a_j<r\cdot u'}} k_{u\to u'}x^u -\sum_{\substack{u\to u'\in R\,:\\r\cdot u'\leq a_j<r\cdot u}} k_{u\to u'}x^u. \end{aligned}$$
- **Capped scale sequence** ([source](#sec:permanence)): Starting at $h_0$, define $$\label{eq:scale-sequence} h_{j+1}=\min\{\sqrt{h_j},h_P\}.$$
- **Classwise absorbing set** ([source](#sec:permanence)): Finally set $$K_P=K_{h_P}\cap(c+S).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-lower | Species concentration | ≥ | $\varepsilon_P$ | The bound depends on the network, fixed rates, and class; entry time depends on the initial point. |
| Q-upper | Species concentration | ≤ | $\varepsilon_P^{-1}$ | The bound depends on the network, fixed rates, and class; entry time depends on the initial point. |
| Q-activity | Active slope approximation error | < | $E(r_\lambda)$ | The tolerance and finite family are fixed before choosing the scale threshold. |
| Q-maximum | Affine minimum | ≤ | $M_h$ | Global in the ambient real space at a scale satisfying the class conditions. |
| Q-tolerance-zero | Tolerance at zero slope | = | $\frac12$ | All nonzero-comparison terms are omitted at zero slope. |
| Q-sign-gap | Nonzero exponent comparison magnitude | ≥ | $H$ | Active slopes in the scale box and the fixed finite comparison family. |
| Q-scale-flux | Scale-weighted total reaction rate | < | $\min_{e\in R}k_e$ | Nonempty reaction set with fixed positive rates and sufficiently small class scale. |
| Q-monomial | Ratio of higher-level to lower-level source monomials | ≤ | $h^H$ | An active slope and its exponent vector in the scale box. |
| Q-directional | Active affine directional derivative | ≥ | $\delta_h$ | Uniform across eligible active labels at one fixed scale, with slopes outside the orthogonal complement. |
| Q-trajectory | Increase along a solution segment | ≥ | $\delta_h(t-s)$ | The entire segment lies in the scale box, in the fixed class, and outside the plateau. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-affine** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine): Induction on dimension, local families at fixed minimum exponent, compact neighborhoods, a finite subcover, and offset shifts. -> A finite affine family with scale-independent slopes.
- **R-uniform** [claim](#stmt-cor-activity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-activity): Contradiction using a repeated active label and a convergent subsequence of exponent vectors. -> Uniform activity estimates below a scale threshold.
- **R-plateau** [claim](#stmt-prop-plateau) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-plateau): Coordinate sign separation forces zero slopes at the reference point; convexity excludes escape beyond the scale box. -> A compact convex maximum plateau contained in the next scale box.
- **R-increase** [claim](#stmt-prop-increase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-increase): Decompose the directional derivative into cut fluxes; return paths supply upward crossings and monomial dominance controls downward flux. -> Uniform positive increase for active slopes outside the stoichiometric orthogonal complement.
- **R-trapping** [claim](#stmt-lem-trapping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trapping): Take the minimum of active directional derivatives, integrate the right-derivative inequality, and use compactness to continue solutions. -> Forward invariance and finite entry for solutions eventually confined to the scale box.
- **R-permanence** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trapping-2): Start in an initial-point-dependent plateau and iterate capped square-root scales using successive box containment. -> A single compact convex forward-invariant absorbing set for the positive class.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **class-permanence**: Each positive stoichiometric class has a common compact convex forward-invariant absorbing set; its entry time may depend on the initial state.
- **cut-flux**: The directional reaction flux equals the sum of signed cut fluxes weighted by the gaps between consecutive complex levels.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **deadline**: Require a common settling deadline for all initial states. -> The transient may depend on the initial state.
- **rate-transfer**: Transfer the same eventual bounds between rate choices or stoichiometric classes. -> The compact set and eventual bounds depend on the fixed rates and class.
- **convergence**: Treat permanence as convergence to a unique equilibrium. -> An absorbing set does not establish convergence to an equilibrium.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The cut-flux decomposition is a finite exact computation for supplied complexes, reactions, slope and state. It checks an algebraic ingredient of the proof; the common absorbing set and eventual entry are not computed.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of finite reaction networks, rational slope, positive rational state and rates.
# PRECONDITIONS: distinct nonnegative integer complexes, valid edge indices, matching dimensions; no approximation of active slopes.
# OUTPUTS: exact rational cut-flux sum for each network; no permanence certificate.
# TIME: polynomial in supplied vertices, edges, dimension and rational bit sizes; bounded input caps.
# SPACE: stored networks and rational intermediate values; bounded input caps.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq exact rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: malformed inputs or violated caps raise an exception; no numerical fallback.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

def evaluate_case(z):
    C, edges = z['C'], z['R']
    r, x = list(map(fmpq, z['r'])), list(map(fmpq, z['x']))
    d = len(r)
    assert 1 <= d <= 8 and 1 <= len(C) <= 16 and len(edges) <= 256
    assert len(x) == d and all(t > 0 for t in x)
    assert len({tuple(y) for y in C}) == len(C)
    assert all(len(y) == d and all(type(v) is int and 0 <= v <= 16 for v in y) for y in C)
    assert all(type(u) is int and type(v) is int and 0 <= u < len(C) and 0 <= v < len(C) and fmpq(k) > 0 for u,v,k in edges)
    heights = [sum((r[i]*y[i] for i in range(d)), fmpq(0)) for y in C]
    levels = sorted(set(heights))
    fluxes = []
    for u,v,k in edges:
        monomial = fmpq(k)
        for i in range(d):
            monomial *= x[i] ** C[u][i]
        fluxes.append((u,v,monomial))
    total = fmpq(0)
    for j in range(len(levels)-1):
        cut = levels[j]
        q = fmpq(0)
        for u,v,flux in fluxes:
            if heights[u] <= cut < heights[v]:
                q += flux
            elif heights[v] <= cut < heights[u]:
                q -= flux
        total += (levels[j+1]-cut)*q
    return str(total)

batch = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
assert isinstance(batch,list) and 1 <= len(batch) <= 1024
print(json.dumps([evaluate_case(z) for z in batch], separators=(',',':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A software model whose continuous state obeys the stated mass-action equation can use classwise permanence as a specification: every positive trajectory eventually enters a common compact forward-invariant set. A reduction from a general software state machine to that equation is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A simulation of a finite weakly reversible mass-action system with fixed positive rates has an exact-model eventual boundedness and positivity contract within each positive stoichiometric class. Preservation of that contract by numerical time stepping or game rules is ABSENT. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | An analysis tool for the stated reaction networks can report the existence of a classwise compact convex absorbing set. The paper does not supply explicit concentration bounds, a numerical error certificate, or an effective procedure for computing the absorbing set from arbitrary input. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument constructs affine minima and uses scale iteration to prove an existence theorem. An executable algorithm with an effective representation of the arbitrary tolerance and offsets, explicit thresholds, and a running-time bound is ABSENT. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite affine family represents a concave minimum and a plateau as an intersection of affine half-spaces. A data-structure interface or bounds for storage, queries, and updates are ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source concerns continuous mass-action trajectories and compact absorbing sets. A hardware computation model, parallel execution contract, or accelerator resource bound is ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Eventual positivity and boundedness within a reaction-network class do not state a security property. A cryptographic construction, adversary model, or security reduction is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A reduction of a learning rule to the stated fixed-rate weakly reversible mass-action dynamics is ABSENT. The permanence conclusion alone does not guarantee learning convergence or an optimisation objective. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Compact convex plateaux are used as trapping sets for reaction-network trajectories. An optimisation problem, objective-value guarantee, or operations-research algorithm is ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A reduction of a distributed protocol to the stated deterministic mass-action equation is ABSENT. Directed return paths in the reaction graph do not by themselves establish protocol safety, termination, or agreement. |
| other (chemical_reaction_networks) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | For finite weakly reversible chemical reaction networks with fixed positive rates, the theorem gives global positive solutions and a common compact convex forward-invariant absorbing set in each positive stoichiometric class, including unbounded classes. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **fixed_positive_mass_action** (hypothesis): The permanence theorem assumes a finite weakly reversible mass-action network with fixed positive reaction rates. Blocked use: ['Extending the permanence theorem to infinite networks, missing directed return paths, non-mass-action dynamics, or rates that vanish or vary with time.']
- **classwise_uniformity** (hypothesis): The absorbing set and eventual concentration bounds are uniform within a fixed positive stoichiometric class and rate choice. Blocked use: ['Transferring the same absorbing set or concentration bounds across distinct classes or rate choices.']
- **initial_state_transient** (edge_case): The entry time may depend on the initial point; permanence supplies eventual containment. Blocked use: ['Claiming a common settling deadline for all initial states.', 'Using eventual concentration bounds as bounds throughout the transient.']
- **positive_initial_class** (hypothesis): The conclusion concerns initial states in a positive stoichiometric class. Blocked use: ['Applying the positive lower bounds to initial states on the boundary of the nonnegative orthant.']
- **existential_scale** (nonconstructive): The proof chooses sufficiently small scales through limiting activity estimates and compactness; it does not supply a general numerical construction of the classwide absorbing set or entry time. Blocked use: ['Treating the existence proof as an executable procedure for calculating certified absorbing bounds and a settling deadline.']
- **no_plateau_nesting** (edge_case): The scale iteration uses containment in successive boxes and does not assume that the plateau sets are nested. Blocked use: ['Replacing the box-containment argument with an unsupported nesting assumption for the plateau sets.']
- **discontinuous_tolerance** (approximation): The affine approximation lemma allows a positive tolerance without regularity and exponent sequences approaching the cube from outside. Blocked use: ['Requiring continuity of the tolerance as a hypothesis.', 'Discarding labels at ties or omitting boundary approaches when implementing the activity argument.']
- **empty_reaction_set** (edge_case): For an empty reaction set, the vector field vanishes and the strict-increase assertion is vacuous. Blocked use: ['Requiring a nonvacuous strictly positive directional derivative when the stoichiometric subspace vanishes.']
- **permanence_not_convergence** (counterexample_scope): The permanence conclusion gives an absorbing invariant set; it does not establish convergence to a unique positive equilibrium for every weakly reversible network. Blocked use: ['Inferring equilibrium uniqueness or asymptotic convergence from weak reversibility and permanence alone.']

          "quote": "Let $C\\subset\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0}^d$ be finite, let $R\\subset C\\times C$ be weakly reversible, and fix positive rate constants in <a href=\"#eq:mass-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-action\">[eq:mass-action]</a>. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the permanence conjecture for finite weakly reversible mass-action systems with fixed positive reaction rates. Every positive stoichiometric compatibility class admits one compact convex forward-invariant set that every positive trajectory in that class enters in finite time, even when the class is unbounded. The set depends only on the network, the rates, and the class; the entry time may depend on the initial state. Thus, after its entry time, every concentration satisfies the same positive lower and finite upper bounds throughout that class.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-lem-affine](#stmt-lem-affine) | lemma | `lem:affine` | 2 | Let $m\geq1$ be an integer, let |
| [stmt-cor-activity](#stmt-cor-activity) | corollary | `cor:activity` | 3 | Let $m\geq1$, $a2. Fix a family $\Lambda$ supplied by that lemma. There is $h_0\in(0,1)$ such that, for every $0<h<h_0$ and every $p\in[a,b]^m$, each label $\lambda\in\Lambda$ active at $X_h(p) |
| [stmt-prop-plateau](#stmt-prop-plateau) | proposition | `prop:plateau` | 4 | For |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Weak reversibility requires every reaction in a chemical reaction network to have a directed return path. It is natural to ask whether this graph condition alone prevents concentrations from approaching zero or infinity. Permanence asks for more: after a transient, all trajectories allowed by the same conservation laws must satisfy the same positive lower and finite upper bounds. We prove this conclusion for arbitrary finite weakly reversible networks with fixed positive reaction rates.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The problem and main result

<a id="eq:mass-action"></a>
Let $d\geq1$. A finite reaction network consists of a set of *complexes* $C\subset\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^d$ and a set of directed *reactions* $R\subset C\times C$. We write $y\to y'$ for $(y,y')\in R$. The network is *weakly reversible* if for every $y\to y'\in R$ there is a directed path from $y'$ to $y$. A *linkage class* is a connected component of the underlying undirected graph. Assign a positive constant $k_{y\to y'}$ to each reaction. The mass-action equation is $$\label{eq:mass-action}
  \dot x=f(x):=\sum_{y\to y'\in R} k_{y\to y'}x^y(y'-y),
  \qquad x^y:=\prod_{i=1}^d x_i^{y_i}.$$ The *stoichiometric subspace* and the *positive stoichiometric class* of $c\in\mathbb R_{>0}^d$ are $$S:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{y'-y:y\to y'\in R\},
  \qquad P:=(c+S)\cap\mathbb R_{>0}^d.$$ Since $f(x)\in S$, any positive solution starting in $P$ remains in $P$ for as long as it exists. The class $P$ need not be bounded.

A global positive trajectory is *persistent* if $\liminf_{t\to\infty}x_i(t)>0$ for every $i$. These lower bounds may depend on the trajectory. We call the system *permanent on $P$* if every solution starting in $P$ is global and positive, and there is one compact set $K\subset P$ that eventually contains each such solution. The entry time may depend on the initial point, but $K$ may not. This is the classwise uniformity in the permanence question (Boros and Hofbauer 2020, Definition 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BorosHofbauer2020](#ref-BorosHofbauer2020)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:uniform-bounds"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Let $C\subset\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^d$ be finite, let $R\subset C\times C$ be weakly reversible, and fix positive rate constants in <a href="#eq:mass-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mass-action">[eq:mass-action]</a>. For every positive stoichiometric class $P$ there is a nonempty compact convex set $K_P\subset P$ with the following properties. Every initial point $x^0\in P$ gives a unique global positive solution, and there is a finite $T(x^0)\geq0$ such that $$x(t)\in K_P\qquad(t\geq T(x^0)).$$ Moreover, $K_P$ is forward invariant. In particular, there is $\varepsilon_P\in(0,1)$, depending only on the network, the fixed rates, and $P$, such that every such solution satisfies $$\label{eq:uniform-bounds}
  \varepsilon_P\leq x_i(t)\leq\varepsilon_P^{-1}
  \qquad(1\leq i\leq d,\ t\geq T(x^0)).$$*

</div>

Thus the fixed-positive-rate, weakly reversible formulation of the permanence conjecture holds, including on unbounded classes. The uniformity in <a href="#eq:uniform-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-bounds">[eq:uniform-bounds]</a> is within each fixed class and rate choice; the transient time is allowed to depend on $x^0$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context and the contribution

The structural theory of mass-action systems begins with the work of Horn and Jackson and of Feinberg (Horn and Jackson 1972; Feinberg 1972) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HornJackson1972](#ref-HornJackson1972), [Feinberg1972](#ref-Feinberg1972)]. A system is *complex-balanced* if it has a positive state at which the total incoming and outgoing reaction fluxes agree at each complex. For a complex-balanced system, the classical theory gives a unique positive equilibrium in each positive stoichiometric class and an entropy-type Lyapunov function. Complex balance imposes conditions on the rates as well as the graph; weak reversibility by itself does not impose these balance identities. Feinberg’s discussion of weakly reversible systems (Feinberg 1987, Remark 6.1.E) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Feinberg1987](#ref-Feinberg1987)] raised the question of excluding limiting states with a zero concentration. Persistence excludes approach to the boundary even along subsequences, and permanence adds upper bounds and uniformity over initial points.

Important partial results developed both the dynamical picture and the geometric methods. Anderson proved that every positive trajectory of a complex-balanced system with one linkage class converges to its class’s positive equilibrium (Anderson 2011, Corollary 4.11) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Anderson2011](#ref-Anderson2011)]. Craciun, Nazarov, and Pantea proved permanence for two-species weakly reversible systems, as part of their more general theorem for endotactic networks (Craciun, Nazarov, and Pantea 2013, Theorem 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CraciunNazarovPantea2013](#ref-CraciunNazarovPantea2013)]. Their endotactic condition expresses an inward-pointing property of the reactions seen in every direction. Pantea proved persistence of bounded weakly reversible trajectories when $\dim S=2$, with no restriction to two species (Pantea 2012, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Pantea2012](#ref-Pantea2012)]. Gopalkrishnan, Miller, and Shiu proved permanence under the geometric condition of strong endotacticity, which includes every weakly reversible network with one linkage class (Gopalkrishnan, Miller, and Shiu 2014, Theorems 1.1 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GopalkrishnanMillerShiu2014](#ref-GopalkrishnanMillerShiu2014)]. Boros and Hofbauer later gave another proof of the single-linkage permanence theorem, producing a compact forward-invariant absorbing set (Boros and Hofbauer 2020, Theorem 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BorosHofbauer2020](#ref-BorosHofbauer2020)]. These permanence results also permit rates varying in time within fixed positive bounds.

Craciun’s toric-differential-inclusion approach constructs invariant regions by comparing monomials in logarithmic coordinates. The revised manuscript of September 23, 2026 proves persistence for bounded positive trajectories of weakly reversible systems, including rates bounded above and away from zero; its complex-balanced application establishes global convergence (Craciun 2026, sec. 8, final remark) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Craciun2026](#ref-Craciun2026)]. The general weakly reversible persistence statement there assumes boundedness of the trajectory. Our theorem treats fixed rates and supplies one eventual bound for every trajectory in a class.

The immediate input to the present argument is the affine approximation lemma and trapping construction of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[OpenAI2026](#ref-OpenAI2026)]. That paper proves that every prescribed positive initial point belongs to a compact convex forward-invariant set contained in the positive orthant. Such sets give boundedness and persistence, but may depend on the initial point. We reproduce the affine lemma and its proof in Section <a href="#sec:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine">2</a>, and adapt the trapping construction in Section <a href="#sec:trapping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:trapping">3</a>. The additional arguments establish strict increase away from a trapping set and connect trapping sets at different scales. They turn bounds for individual trajectories into permanence on a fixed class. Monomial comparisons and invariant regions have the earlier antecedents just described; the affine approximation lemma is credited to (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 2.1 and Corollary 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAI2026](#ref-OpenAI2026)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof overview

Fix one representative $c\in P$. For $0<h<1$, set $B_h=[h,h^{-1}]^d$. Every $x\in B_h$ has a unique representation $x=(h^{p_1},\ldots,h^{p_d})$ with $p\in[-1,1]^d$. The affine lemma constructs a finite concave minimum $$F_h(x)=\min_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
     \bigl(r_\lambda\cdot x+b_\lambda(h)\bigr),$$ whose slopes $r_\lambda\in[-1,1]^d$ are independent of $h$. A label $\lambda$ is *active* at $x$ when it attains this minimum. For any prescribed tolerance $E:[-1,1]^d\to(0,\infty)$, the construction ensures $\left\|r_\lambda-p\right\|_\infty<E(r_\lambda)$ for every active label, uniformly for small $h$. We choose the tolerance so that every nonzero comparison $r_\lambda\cdot v$, where $v$ is a coordinate vector or a difference of distinct complexes, has the same sign as $p\cdot v$. To preserve these signs, our tolerance shrinks near a hyperplane $r\cdot v=0$; on the hyperplane, the zero comparison imposes no restriction. This is why the affine lemma allows a discontinuous tolerance. When $r_\lambda\cdot v\ne0$, finiteness of the slope family also gives a uniform positive lower bound for $\left|p\cdot v\right|$.

At sufficiently small scales, the labels active at $c$ have zero slope, so $F_h(c)$ is the global maximum of $F_h$. Its plateau $$K_h:=\{x\in\mathbb R^d:F_h(x)=F_h(c)\}$$ is compact and convex, contains $c$, and satisfies $K_h\subset\operatorname{int}B_{\sqrt h}$ (Proposition <a href="#prop:plateau" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:plateau">4</a>). The restriction to $P$ now supplies the decisive strictness condition. A member of the affine family whose slope lies in $S^\perp$ is constant on $c+S$, so its value on $P$ is at least $F_h(c)$. Such a label cannot be active at a point of $P\setminus K_h$.

For any active label $\lambda$, order the complexes by the levels $r_\lambda\cdot y$, and separate consecutive levels by cuts. If a downward reaction crosses a cut, its directed return path contains an upward crossing. Monomials at lower source levels dominate those at higher levels, so one upward flux exceeds the entire downward flux for sufficiently small $h$. The net upward flux across each cut is therefore nonnegative, and is positive whenever a reaction crosses it. Summing these fluxes times the gaps between levels gives $r_\lambda\cdot f(x)\geq0$, with strict inequality when $r_\lambda\notin S^\perp$. Compactness at each fixed scale makes the strict inequality uniform over all active labels to which it applies (Proposition <a href="#prop:increase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:increase">5</a>). Thus $F_h$ is nondecreasing along solution segments in $B_h$ and increases at a fixed positive rate on $B_h\cap(P\setminus K_h)$. A trajectory in $P$ that stays in $B_h$ for all later times must reach $K_h$ in finite time and remain there.

Every initial point in $P$ belongs to some $K_h$, giving global existence first. The inclusion $K_h\subset B_{\sqrt h}$ then permits passage to a larger scale. Repeated square roots, capped at a scale $h_P$ fixed by the class, reach $h_P$ after finitely many steps. Section <a href="#sec:permanence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:permanence">4</a> carries out this iteration and obtains $K_P=K_{h_P}\cap(c+S)$, with $h_P$ chosen independently of the initial point.

<a id="sec:affine"></a>
### Affine minima with arbitrary tolerance

The proof will use a finite minimum of affine functions on the positive orthant. Its essential property is that an active slope approximates the exponent vector of the point where it is active. The allowed error will depend on the slope and may be discontinuous. We prove the construction and its uniform consequence from (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 2.1 and Corollary 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OpenAI2026](#ref-OpenAI2026)] in full.

For $m\geq1$, $u\in\mathbb R^m$, and $0<h<1$, put $$X_h(u)=(h^{u_1},\ldots,h^{u_m}).$$ A *label* $\lambda=(r_\lambda,b_\lambda)$ consists of a slope $r_\lambda\in\mathbb R^m$, independent of $h$, and an offset $b_\lambda(h)$ defined for all sufficiently small positive $h$. For a finite nonempty family $\Lambda$ of labels, write $$F_h(x)=\min_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
       \bigl(r_\lambda\cdot x+b_\lambda(h)\bigr).$$ A label is *active at $x$* if its affine value equals $F_h(x)$. Every minimizing label is active when there is a tie. All offsets in a finite family are understood to be restricted to a common interval on which they are defined.

<a id="stmt-lem-affine"></a>
<div id="lem:affine" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Affine approximation with arbitrary tolerance). *Let $m\geq1$ be an integer, let $a<b$ be real numbers, and let $E:[a,b]^m\to(0,\infty)$ be any function. There is a finite nonempty family $\Lambda$ of labels with $$r_\lambda\in[a,b]^m,
 \qquad b_\lambda(h)=o(h^a)\quad(h\to0+),$$ such that the following implication holds. If $h_\nu\to0+$, $p^\nu\in\mathbb R^m$ converges to $p\in[a,b]^m$, and a fixed label $\lambda\in\Lambda$ is active at $X_{h_\nu}(p^\nu)$ for every $\nu$, then $$\left\|p-r_\lambda\right\|_\infty<E(r_\lambda).$$ The slopes and the family are independent of $h$. No regularity is assumed of $E$, and the approximating vectors $p^\nu$ need not lie in $[a,b]^m$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-affine"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-affine</summary>

*Proof.* We induct on $m$. Write $\mu(u)=\min_i u_i$ and $\mathbf 1=(1,\ldots,1)$. Since the largest coordinate of $X_h(u)$ is $h^{\mu(u)}$, we first construct a family for each fixed value $t$ of the smallest exponent. We then extend its guarantee to a neighborhood of $t$ and combine finitely many such families by shifting their offsets. The fixed-minimum construction is a singleton when $m=1$; the extension and combination therefore also prove the base case.

Throughout the proof, asymptotic comparisons along $h_\nu\to0+$ and $p^\nu\to p$ use fixed gaps between limiting exponents. In particular, if $p_i>q$, then eventually $p_i^\nu\geq q+\delta$ for some $\delta>0$, so $h_\nu^{p_i^\nu}=o(h_\nu^q)$; if $p_i<q$, the ratio $h_\nu^{p_i^\nu}/h_\nu^q$ tends to infinity. We shall use these comparisons only with strict limiting gaps.

*A family for a fixed minimum.* Fix $t\in[a,b]$. We construct a finite family $\Lambda_t$ containing $(t\mathbf 1,0)$, with $\mu(r)=t$ for every slope, such that the conclusion of the lemma holds for activity within $\Lambda_t$ whenever $\mu(p)=t$.

If $m=1$ or $t=b$, the family $\{(t\mathbf 1,0)\}$ suffices: a point $p\in[a,b]^m$ with $\mu(p)=t$ then equals $t\mathbf 1$. Otherwise $m>1$ and $t<b$. Say a label has *type $k$* if exactly $k$ entries of its slope exceed $t$, the remaining entries being $t$. Start with the type $0$ label $(t\mathbf 1,0)$, and construct types $k=1,\ldots,m-1$ successively. After type $k-1$ has been constructed, choose $\beta_k$ satisfying $$\begin{gathered}
 t<\beta_k<b,\qquad
 \beta_k<\beta_{k-1}\quad\text{if }k>1,\\
 \beta_k-t<E(r)
 \quad\text{for every slope $r$ of type $k-1$.}
\end{gathered}$$ These conditions use only finitely many positive values of $E$, so such a choice is possible. The new type $k$ labels will force every limiting exponent on a coordinate left at $t$ by a type $k-1$ label that stays active to be at most $\beta_k$. The last inequality will then bound the error by that label’s tolerance.

<a id="eq:local-labels"></a>
For each $k$-element coordinate set $U\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}$ and $s\in[\beta_k,b]^U$, let $r(s)$ equal $s$ on $U$ and $t$ off $U$. Apply the induction hypothesis in dimension $k$ to this cube with tolerance $s\mapsto E(r(s))$, identifying $U$ with its naturally ordered coordinates. For every resulting label $(s,d)$, add to $\Lambda_t$ the type $k$ label $$r=r(s),\qquad c(h)=-h^{\beta_k}+d(h),
 \qquad d(h)=o(h^{\beta_k}).
 \label{eq:local-labels}$$ Here and below $c$ denotes a local offset. The resulting family is finite, and all its offsets are $o(h^t)$. The negative offset favors raising slope entries on coordinates where $X_h(p)$ is small; the decreasing $\beta_k$ make that preference stronger with each successive type.

<a id="eq:raised-coordinates"></a>
To verify the guarantee, let $p^\nu\to p\in[a,b]^m$ with $\mu(p)=t$, and suppose a type $k$ label $(r,c)$ stays active within $\Lambda_t$ along $h_\nu\to0+$. Let $U$ be its raised coordinate set, and abbreviate $h=h_\nu$. For $k>0$, comparison with $(t\mathbf 1,0)$ gives $$\sum_{i\in U}(r_i-t)h^{p_i^\nu}
 \leq h^{\beta_k}-d(h)=(1+o(1))h^{\beta_k}.$$ Every coefficient on the left is at least $\beta_k-t>0$. A limiting exponent $p_i<\beta_k$ would make the corresponding term divided by $h^{\beta_k}$ tend to infinity. Therefore $$p_i\geq\beta_k\qquad(i\in U).
 \label{eq:raised-coordinates}$$ Among labels made from this same $U$, all contributions off $U$ and the term $-h^{\beta_k}$ are common. Thus $(s,d)$ is active in the family furnished by induction. Its limiting exponent vector lies in $[\beta_k,b]^U$, so $$\max_{i\in U}\left|p_i-r_i\right|<E(r).$$ The approximating vectors $p_U^\nu$ may approach a face of that cube from outside. This is why the induction statement allows them outside the cube, including when $p_i=\beta_k$ or $p_i=b$.

It remains to bound the coordinates off $U$. This argument also includes type $0$, for which $U=\varnothing$. If $k<m-1$, we claim that $$p_i\leq\beta_{k+1}\qquad(i\notin U).$$ Otherwise choose $j\notin U$ with $p_j>\beta_{k+1}$ and any type $k+1$ label raised on $U\cup\{j\}$. Every limiting exponent on this set exceeds $\beta_{k+1}$, by <a href="#eq:raised-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:raised-coordinates">[eq:raised-coordinates]</a> and $\beta_k>\beta_{k+1}$ when $k>0$. The competitor’s affine value minus that of $(t\mathbf 1,0)$ is therefore $$-h^{\beta_{k+1}}+o(h^{\beta_{k+1}}).$$ For the supposed active label, the same difference is zero if $k=0$, and is bounded below by $-h^{\beta_k}+o(h^{\beta_k})$ if $k>0$. Since $h^{\beta_k}=o(h^{\beta_{k+1}})$, the competitor is strictly smaller for all sufficiently small $h$ along the sequence, a contradiction. Consequently, off $U$, $$\left|p_i-r_i\right|\leq\beta_{k+1}-t<E(r).$$ If $k=m-1$, there is just one coordinate off $U$. Its limiting exponent is $t$, since $\mu(p)=t$ and all coordinates on $U$ have limiting exponents greater than $t$. Its error is zero. This proves the required strict bound for every active label at the fixed minimum.

*Extension to nearby minima.* For a fixed label $\lambda=(r,c)\in\Lambda_t$, let $A_\lambda\subset[a,b]^m$ consist of the limits of all sequences $p^\nu\in\mathbb R^m$ for which $h_\nu\to0+$ and $\lambda$ is active within $\Lambda_t$ at $X_{h_\nu}(p^\nu)$. This set is closed. Indeed, if $z_n\in A_\lambda$ and $z_n\to z$, choose from a sequence realizing $z_n$ a parameter $0<h_n<1/n$ and a vector $u_n$ with $\left\|u_n-z_n\right\|_\infty<1/n$ such that $\lambda$ is active at $X_{h_n}(u_n)$. Then $u_n\to z$, so this selected sequence realizes $z\in A_\lambda$. This argument uses actual active points and requires no continuity of the offsets in $h$.

The set of limits violating the desired estimate, $$B_t=\bigcup_{\lambda=(r,c)\in\Lambda_t}
 \{p\in A_\lambda:\left\|p-r\right\|_\infty\geq E(r)\},$$ is compact: the union is finite and each $E(r)$ here is a fixed number. The preceding argument shows that $B_t$ contains no point with $\mu(p)=t$. Continuity of $\mu$ and compactness therefore give $\rho_t>0$ such that $B_t$ contains no point with $$\mu(p)\in I_t:=(t-\rho_t,t+\rho_t).$$ If $B_t$ is empty any positive radius initially suffices; otherwise take $\rho_t<\min_{p\in B_t}\left|\mu(p)-t\right|$. Thus $\Lambda_t$ has the asserted limiting activity property whenever $\mu(p)\in I_t$.

<a id="eq:local-decay"></a>
Shrink $\rho_t$ further, if necessary, so that every local offset obeys $$c(h)=o(h^{t+\rho_t}).
 \label{eq:local-decay}$$ For a nonsingleton family, choose $t+\rho_t<\min_k\beta_k$ and use <a href="#eq:local-labels" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-labels">[eq:local-labels]</a>; for a singleton the offset is zero. We have obtained a family valid near each possible minimum exponent. The remaining task is to ensure that a globally active label comes from a family valid at its own limiting point.

*Combining the families.* Take a finite subcover of $[a,b]$ from the intervals $I_t$. Discard intervals contained in others, keeping just one if two coincide, and order the remaining centers as $t_1<\cdots<t_N$. Write $I_{t_j}=(L_j,R_j)$. Noncontainment forces both endpoints to have the same strict order; since $t_j=(L_j+R_j)/2$, it is the order of the centers. Hence $$L_1<\cdots<L_N,\qquad R_1<\cdots<R_N.$$ Consecutive intervals overlap. If $R_j\leq L_{j+1}$, a point of $[R_j,L_{j+1}]\subset(t_j,t_{j+1})\subset[a,b]$ would be uncovered. The first interval contains $a$ and the last contains $b$.

<a id="eq:affine-cuts"></a>
For $1\leq j<N$, choose a cut $$q_j\in
 \bigl(\max\{t_j,L_{j+1}\},\min\{t_{j+1},R_j\}\bigr).$$ The interval displayed is nonempty because consecutive intervals overlap and each contains its own center. With $q_0=a$ and $q_N=b$, we have $$t_j<q_j<t_{j+1}\quad(1\leq j<N),
 \qquad [q_{j-1},q_j]\subset I_{t_j}\quad(1\leq j\leq N).
 \label{eq:affine-cuts}$$ For $N=1$, the sole interval contains $[a,b]$ and no interior cuts are needed. The closed intervals in <a href="#eq:affine-cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:affine-cuts">[eq:affine-cuts]</a> are the ranges of minimum exponents assigned to the respective families; see Figure <a href="#fig:intervals" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:intervals">1</a>.

<a id="eq:affine-shifts"></a>
Combine the selected families, replacing every local offset $c(h)$ in $\Lambda_{t_j}$ by $$\widetilde c(h)=c(h)-\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}h^{q_\ell}.
 \label{eq:affine-shifts}$$ These offsets are $o(h^a)$: <a href="#eq:local-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-decay">[eq:local-decay]</a> has $t_j+\rho_{t_j}>a$, and each shift exponent satisfies $q_\ell>t_\ell\geq a$. A common shift preserves activity within each family. Between consecutive families, the new term $-h^{q_j}$ will force any limiting minimum exponent to the correct side of the cut.

<figure id="fig:intervals">

<figcaption>Combining three local families, schematically. Each thick closed interval $[q_{j − 1},q_{j}]$ lies inside the open neighborhood $I_{tj}$ where its family is valid. The shifts in <a href="#eq:affine-shifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:affine-shifts">[eq:affine-shifts]</a> force the limiting minimum exponent of an active label into this closed interval. At a cut, either neighboring family remains valid.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-intervals"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:intervals`)

  \foreach \x/\label in {0.6/{a=q_0},2/{t_1},3.5/{q_1},5/{t_2},6.5/{q_2},8/{t_3},9.5/{b=q_3}} {

Visual witness description of PDF page 7 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A horizontal axis at the bottom with an arrow pointing to the right labeled $\mu(p)$. Ticks on the axis are labeled from left to right: $a = q_0$, $t_1$, $q_1$, $t_2$, $q_2$, $t_3$, $b = q_3$. Above the axis, three horizontal intervals are depicted at descending vertical levels:
- Top level: an open line segment with open circle endpoints labeled $I_{t_1}$ at the right; contains a thick line segment spanning from $a = q_0$ to $q_1$, with vertical dashed lines extending down from its endpoints to the axis ticks at $a = q_0$ and $q_1$.
- Middle level: an open line segment with open circle endpoints labeled $I_{t_2}$ at the right; contains a thick line segment spanning from $q_1$ to $q_2$, with vertical dashed lines extending down from its endpoints to the axis ticks at $q_1$ and $q_2$.
- Bottom level: an open line segment with open circle endpoints labeled $I_{t_3}$ at the right; contains a thick line segment spanning from $q_2$ to $b = q_3$, with vertical dashed lines extending down from its endpoints to the axis ticks at $q_2$ and $b = q_3$.
```


Suppose a fixed label from $\Lambda_{t_j}$ is active in the combined family along $h_\nu\to0+$ and $p^\nu\to p\in[a,b]^m$. Write $c(h)$ for its original local offset and $h=h_\nu$. We claim $$q_{j-1}\leq\mu(p)\leq q_j.$$ If $j<N$ and $\mu(p)>q_j$, each coordinate $h^{p_i^\nu}$ is $o(h^{q_j})$. Also $c(h)=o(h^{q_j})$, by <a href="#eq:local-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-decay">[eq:local-decay]</a> and $q_j<R_j=t_j+\rho_{t_j}$. Subtracting the value of the shifted label $(t_{j+1}\mathbf 1,0)$ from the active label’s value gives $$(r-t_{j+1}\mathbf 1)\cdot X_h(p^\nu)+c(h)+h^{q_j}
   =h^{q_j}+o(h^{q_j})>0,$$ a contradiction.

If $j>1$ and $\mu(p)<q_{j-1}$, compare instead with the shifted label $(t_{j-1}\mathbf 1,0)$. The difference is $$(r-t_{j-1}\mathbf 1)\cdot X_h(p^\nu)+c(h)-h^{q_{j-1}}.$$ Every coefficient in the scalar product is at least $t_j-t_{j-1}>0$. Some coordinate has $p_i<q_{j-1}$, so its exponential divided by $h^{q_{j-1}}$ tends to infinity. Meanwhile, $c(h)=o(h^{q_{j-1}})$ because $q_{j-1}<t_j<t_j+\rho_{t_j}$. Thus the displayed difference divided by $h^{q_{j-1}}$ tends to $+\infty$, again contradicting activity. The outer bounds for $j=1$ or $j=N$ follow from $\mu(p)\in[a,b]$.

By <a href="#eq:affine-cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:affine-cuts">[eq:affine-cuts]</a>, the claimed bounds place $\mu(p)$ inside $I_{t_j}$, including when it equals a cut. The label is active within its original local family, so the guarantee on $I_{t_j}$ gives $\left\|p-r\right\|_\infty<E(r)$. This completes the induction. ◻

</details>

The sequential formulation yields the uniform statement needed below. Its order of choices is important: the family, and hence its finite set of slopes, is fixed before the upper bound on $h$ is chosen.

<a id="stmt-cor-activity"></a>
<div id="cor:activity" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Uniform activity bound). *Let $m\geq1$, $a<b$, and $E:[a,b]^m\to(0,\infty)$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:affine">2</a>. Fix a family $\Lambda$ supplied by that lemma. There is $h_0\in(0,1)$ such that, for every $0<h<h_0$ and every $p\in[a,b]^m$, each label $\lambda\in\Lambda$ active at $X_h(p)$ satisfies $$\left\|p-r_\lambda\right\|_\infty<E(r_\lambda).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-activity"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-activity</summary>

*Proof.* Otherwise there are $h_\nu\to0+$, points $p^\nu\in[a,b]^m$, and active labels $\lambda_\nu$ such that $\left\|p^\nu-r_{\lambda_\nu}\right\|_\infty\geq E(r_{\lambda_\nu})$. By finiteness and compactness, pass to a subsequence on which $\lambda_\nu=\lambda$ is fixed and $p^\nu\to p\in[a,b]^m$. The inequality passes to the limit and contradicts Lemma <a href="#lem:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:affine">2</a>. No continuity of $E$ is needed because its argument $r_\lambda$ is fixed on this subsequence. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:trapping"></a>
### Compact plateaux and strict increase

We now apply Corollary <a href="#cor:activity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:activity">3</a> to the reaction network. For each sufficiently small scale we will construct a compact set in the positive orthant that is forward invariant. For a fixed positive stoichiometric class, we will also prove that a trajectory in that class remaining in the scale’s box reaches this set in finite time. The affine minimum and sign argument adapt the trapping construction of (OpenAI 2026, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[OpenAI2026](#ref-OpenAI2026)]; the strict increase away from a plateau is the additional property needed for finite entry within a fixed class.

<a id="eq:network-activity"></a>
Use $X_h$ from Section <a href="#sec:affine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine">2</a> with $m=d$, and for $0<h<1$ put $$B_h=[h,h^{-1}]^d.$$ Every $x\in B_h$ is uniquely $X_h(p)$ for $p\in[-1,1]^d$, with $p=\log x/\log h$, where the logarithm of a vector is taken coordinatewise. We first choose the tolerance in Corollary <a href="#cor:activity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:activity">3</a> so that active slopes determine both coordinate signs and the ordering of monomials. Let <a id="eq:comparison-directions"></a>$$\label{eq:comparison-directions}
 D=\{e_i:1\leq i\leq d\}
   \cup\{z-y:y,z\in C,\ z\ne y\},$$ where $e_i$ is the $i$th coordinate vector. This is a finite set of nonzero vectors. We include all pairs of complexes because the flux estimate will compare sources of different reactions, possibly in different components of the reaction graph. Define, for $r\in[-1,1]^d$, <a id="eq:network-tolerance"></a>$$\label{eq:network-tolerance}
 E(r)=\min\left(
 \left\{\frac12\right\}\cup
 \left\{\frac{\left|r\cdot v\right|}{2\left\|v\right\|_1}:
                 v\in D,\ r\cdot v\ne0\right\}\right).$$ Zero scalar products impose no sign restriction and are omitted. Thus $E$ is positive at every point, including on the comparison hyperplanes; no continuity is needed. Fix the finite nonempty family supplied by Corollary <a href="#cor:activity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:activity">3</a> for this $E$, and write its affine functions as $$L_{\lambda,h}(x)=r_\lambda\cdot x+b_\lambda(h),
 \qquad
 F_h(x)=\min_{\lambda\in\Lambda}L_{\lambda,h}(x).$$ The slopes $r_\lambda\in[-1,1]^d$ and the index set $\Lambda$ are fixed independently of $h$. A label $\lambda$ is active at $x$ if $L_{\lambda,h}(x)=F_h(x)$; denote the set of these labels by $A_h(x)$. For every sufficiently small $h$, the activity estimate is $$\label{eq:network-activity}
 x=X_h(p)\in B_h,\quad \lambda\in A_h(x)
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \left\|p-r_\lambda\right\|_\infty<E(r_\lambda).$$

<a id="eq:sign-and-size"></a>
Because the family of slopes is fixed and finite, the number <a id="eq:uniform-sign-gap"></a>$$\label{eq:uniform-sign-gap}
 H=\min\left(
 \{1\}\cup
 \left\{\frac{\left|r_\lambda\cdot v\right|}2:
       \lambda\in\Lambda,\ v\in D,\ r_\lambda\cdot v\ne0\right\}\right)$$ is positive. If $x=X_h(p)\in B_h$, $\lambda\in A_h(x)$, and $r=r_\lambda$, then $$\left|(p-r)\cdot v\right|
 \leq\left\|p-r\right\|_\infty\left\|v\right\|_1
 <E(r)\left\|v\right\|_1
 \leq\frac{\left|r\cdot v\right|}2
 \qquad (v\in D,\ r\cdot v\ne0).$$ Consequently, $$\label{eq:sign-and-size}
 r\cdot v\ne0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \operatorname{sgn}(p\cdot v)=\operatorname{sgn}(r\cdot v),
 \qquad \left|p\cdot v\right|\geq H
 \qquad (v\in D).$$

<a id="eq:class-scale"></a>
Fix a positive stoichiometric class $P$, and choose one representative $c\in P$ once for this class. Choose $h_P\in(0,1)$ so small that the affine functions are defined and <a href="#eq:network-activity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:network-activity">[eq:network-activity]</a> holds for every $0<h\leq h_P$, and also $$\label{eq:class-scale}
 \left\|\frac{\log c}{\log h}\right\|_\infty<H,
 \qquad
 h^H\sum_{e\in R}k_e<\min_{e\in R}k_e
 \quad (0<h\leq h_P).$$ The second inequality is imposed only when $R\ne\varnothing$. Such a choice exists because $H>0$, all the rates are positive, and both $1/\left|\log h\right|$ and $h^H$ tend to zero as $h$ tends to zero. In particular, $c\in\operatorname{int}B_h$ for these scales. All the remaining statements of this section concern $0<h\leq h_P$.

<a id="stmt-prop-plateau"></a>
<div id="prop:plateau" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:plateau-containment"></a>
**Proposition 4** (The compact plateau). *For $0<h\leq h_P$, let $$M_h=F_h(c),
 \qquad
 K_h=\{x\in\mathbb R^d:F_h(x)=M_h\}.$$ There is a constant label with value $M_h$, and $F_h\leq M_h$ on $\mathbb R^d$. The set $K_h$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, and $$\label{eq:plateau-containment}
 K_h\subset\operatorname{int}B_{\sqrt h}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-plateau"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-plateau</summary>

*Proof.* Write $p_c=\log c/\log h$. If a label active at $c$ had a nonzero coordinate $r_{\lambda,i}$, <a href="#eq:sign-and-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sign-and-size">[eq:sign-and-size]</a> with $v=e_i$ would give $\left|p_{c,i}\right|\geq H$, contrary to <a href="#eq:class-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:class-scale">[eq:class-scale]</a>. Thus every label active at $c$ has zero slope. At least one label is active there, so one of the affine functions is the constant $M_h$. It follows that $F_h\leq M_h$ everywhere, and that $M_h$ is the least value of any constant label. Hence $$K_h=\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
       \{x\in\mathbb R^d:L_{\lambda,h}(x)\geq M_h\}$$ is closed and convex, and contains $c$.

If $x=X_h(p)\in K_h\cap B_h$, the constant label with value $M_h$ is active at $x$. Applying <a href="#eq:network-activity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:network-activity">[eq:network-activity]</a> to its zero slope gives $$\left\|p\right\|_\infty<E(0)=\frac12,
 \qquad\text{and thus}\qquad
 x\in\operatorname{int}B_{\sqrt h}.$$ In particular, $K_h$ misses the boundary of $B_h$. If some point of $K_h$ lay outside $B_h$, the segment joining it to $c\in\operatorname{int}B_h$ would meet that boundary at a point of $K_h$, by convexity. This is impossible. Thus $K_h\subset B_h$, and the preceding argument proves <a href="#eq:plateau-containment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:plateau-containment">[eq:plateau-containment]</a> for every point of $K_h$. Closedness and boundedness give compactness. ◻

</details>

The plateau is now a compact candidate for a trapping set. A slope in $S^\perp$ gives an affine function that is constant on $P$. We first exclude such slopes below the plateau, then compare reaction fluxes to prove strict increase for every remaining active label.

<a id="stmt-prop-increase"></a>
<div id="prop:increase" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:active-flux-sign"></a>
<a id="eq:uniform-active-increase"></a>
**Proposition 5** (Increase of active affine functions). *Fix $0<h\leq h_P$. For every $x\in B_h$ and every $\lambda\in A_h(x)$, $$\label{eq:active-flux-sign}
 r_\lambda\cdot f(x)\geq0,
 \qquad
 r_\lambda\cdot f(x)>0\quad\text{if }r_\lambda\notin S^\perp.$$ Moreover, there is a number $\delta_h>0$ such that $$\label{eq:uniform-active-increase}
 r_\lambda\cdot f(x)\geq\delta_h
 \quad\text{whenever }x\in B_h,\ \lambda\in A_h(x),
                       \ r_\lambda\notin S^\perp.$$ At every point of $P\setminus K_h$, all active slopes lie outside $S^\perp$. In particular, <a href="#eq:uniform-active-increase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-active-increase">[eq:uniform-active-increase]</a> applies to every active label at each $x\in B_h\cap(P\setminus K_h)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-increase"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-increase</summary>

*Proof.* First, let $x\in P\setminus K_h$. If $r_\lambda\in S^\perp$, then $x-c\in S$ implies $$L_{\lambda,h}(x)=L_{\lambda,h}(c)\geq F_h(c)=M_h.$$ But $F_h(x)<M_h$ by Proposition <a href="#prop:plateau" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:plateau">4</a>. Such a label cannot be active at $x$.

<a id="eq:monomial-order"></a>
If $R$ is empty, then $f=0$ and $S=\{0\}$, so <a href="#eq:active-flux-sign" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-flux-sign">[eq:active-flux-sign]</a> holds and <a href="#eq:uniform-active-increase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-active-increase">[eq:uniform-active-increase]</a> is vacuous. For the flux comparison, assume $R\ne\varnothing$ and fix $x=X_h(p)\in B_h$ and an active slope $r$. Whenever $y,z\in C$ satisfy $r\cdot y<r\cdot z$, <a href="#eq:sign-and-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sign-and-size">[eq:sign-and-size]</a> applied to $z-y$ gives $$\label{eq:monomial-order}
 \frac{x^z}{x^y}=h^{p\cdot(z-y)}\leq h^H.$$ List the distinct values of $r\cdot y$, for $y\in C$, as $a_1<\cdots<a_m$. For $1\leq j<m$, define the net upward flux across the cut at $a_j$ by $$\begin{aligned}
 Q_j(x)={}&
 \sum_{\substack{u\to u'\in R\,:\\r\cdot u\leq a_j<r\cdot u'}}
       k_{u\to u'}x^u
 -\sum_{\substack{u\to u'\in R\,:\\r\cdot u'\leq a_j<r\cdot u}}
       k_{u\to u'}x^u.
\end{aligned}$$ An edge crossing the cut in the first sum is called upward, and an edge in the second sum is called downward.

If there is a downward edge $u\to u'$, weak reversibility supplies a directed path from $u'$ back to $u$. This path starts below the cut and ends above it, so it contains an upward edge, say $w\to w'$. Every source $u$ of a downward edge has $r\cdot u>r\cdot w$. By <a href="#eq:monomial-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monomial-order">[eq:monomial-order]</a> and <a href="#eq:class-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:class-scale">[eq:class-scale]</a>, $$\sum_{\substack{u\to u'\in R\,:\\r\cdot u'\leq a_j<r\cdot u}}
       k_{u\to u'}x^u
 \leq h^H x^w\sum_{e\in R}k_e
 < k_{w\to w'}x^w.$$ Thus one upward edge already contributes more than the entire downward sum, and $Q_j(x)>0$. If there is no downward edge, then $Q_j(x)\geq0$, with strict inequality whenever there is an upward edge. In all cases $Q_j(x)$ is nonnegative, and it is positive if any reaction crosses the cut.

The contribution of an edge to $r\cdot f(x)$ is its flux multiplied by the difference between the levels of its target and source. Writing this difference as the signed sum of the intervening gaps gives <a id="eq:cut-flux-decomposition"></a>$$\label{eq:cut-flux-decomposition}
 r\cdot f(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}(a_{j+1}-a_j)Q_j(x).$$ Every term is nonnegative. If $r\notin S^\perp$, some reaction $y\to y'$ satisfies $r\cdot(y'-y)\ne0$ and therefore crosses at least one cut; the corresponding term is positive. This proves <a href="#eq:active-flux-sign" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:active-flux-sign">[eq:active-flux-sign]</a>. When $m=1$, the sum is empty and every reaction has zero scalar product with $r$, as required. Self-loops never cross a cut and contribute zero to the vector field.

For the uniform bound, the active set of a fixed label, $$T_{\lambda,h}=
 \{x\in B_h:L_{\lambda,h}(x)=F_h(x)\},$$ is compact. If $r_\lambda\notin S^\perp$ and $T_{\lambda,h}$ is nonempty, the continuous function $r_\lambda\cdot f$ is strictly positive on this set and has a positive minimum there. Take the minimum of these positive numbers over the finitely many eligible labels. If there are none, choose any $\delta_h>0$. This proves <a href="#eq:uniform-active-increase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-active-increase">[eq:uniform-active-increase]</a>, including the case $R=\varnothing$. ◻

</details>

The strict inequality in Proposition <a href="#prop:increase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:increase">5</a> is uniform at a fixed scale even near the plateau. Together with the finite nature of the affine minimum, this gives a direct finite-time trapping statement.

<a id="stmt-lem-trapping"></a>
<div id="lem:trapping" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:trajectory-increase"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Trapping and finite entry). *Fix $0<h\leq h_P$. Along any solution segment contained in $B_h$, the function $t\mapsto F_h(x(t))$ is nondecreasing. If the segment $x([s,t])$ is contained in $B_h\cap(P\setminus K_h)$, then $$\label{eq:trajectory-increase}
 F_h(x(t))-F_h(x(s))\geq\delta_h(t-s).$$ Every solution starting in $K_h$ exists for all nonnegative time and remains in $K_h$. In particular, solutions starting in $K_h\cap P$ remain in $K_h\cap P$. Finally, any global solution in $P$ that stays in $B_h$ from some time onward enters $K_h$ in finite time and then remains there.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-trapping"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-trapping</summary>

<a id="eq:right-derivative"></a>
*Proof.* For a solution $x(t)$ and each fixed time $t$ before its maximal existence time, the right derivative of its affine minimum is $$\label{eq:right-derivative}
 \frac{d^+}{dt}F_h(x(t))
   =\min_{\lambda\in A_h(x(t))}r_\lambda\cdot f(x(t)).$$ Indeed, all inactive labels have a positive gap above the minimum at time $t$. There are finitely many of them, so continuity excludes them from the minimum for a sufficiently short time to the right. For each active label, differentiability of the solution gives $$L_{\lambda,h}(x(t+s))
 =F_h(x(t))+s\,r_\lambda\cdot f(x(t))+o(s)
 \qquad(s\downarrow0).$$ Taking the minimum of these finitely many expansions proves <a href="#eq:right-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:right-derivative">[eq:right-derivative]</a>.

We use the elementary fact that a continuous function $g$ on $[a,b]$ with right derivative at least $q$ at every point of $[a,b)$ satisfies $g(b)-g(a)\geq q(b-a)$. For completeness, for any $\eta>0$ the function $g(u)-qu+\eta u$ has positive right derivative before $b$. Its maximum on the compact interval therefore cannot occur before $b$. Comparing its values at $a$ and $b$ and then letting $\eta\downarrow0$ proves the assertion. Formula <a href="#eq:right-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:right-derivative">[eq:right-derivative]</a> and Proposition <a href="#prop:increase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:increase">5</a> now give monotonicity in $B_h$, and give <a href="#eq:trajectory-increase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trajectory-increase">[eq:trajectory-increase]</a> when the segment lies in $P\setminus K_h$.

Suppose next that $x(0)\in K_h$, and consider the maximal solution of the polynomial vector field $f$. Since $K_h\subset\operatorname{int}B_h$, the solution initially stays in $B_h$. On any interval on which it does so, monotonicity and the global upper bound $F_h\leq M_h$ force $F_h(x(t))=M_h$. Hence it remains in $K_h$. It cannot have a first exit from $\operatorname{int}B_h$ within its interval of existence: continuity would place the exit point in both $K_h$ and the boundary of $B_h$, contradicting Proposition <a href="#prop:plateau" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:plateau">4</a>. Thus the solution stays in the compact set $K_h$ throughout its maximal interval. A solution of a smooth vector field that remains in a compact subset of its domain extends past any finite endpoint of its interval of existence. To see the relevant point here, $f$ is bounded on a neighborhood of $K_h$, so a finite endpoint would give a limit of $x(t)$ in $K_h$, at which local existence continues the solution. The solution is therefore global. It remains positive by <a href="#eq:plateau-containment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:plateau-containment">[eq:plateau-containment]</a>; moreover, $f(x)\in S$ implies $x(t)-x(0)\in S$, so a solution starting in $P$ stays in $P$.

Finally, let a global solution in $P$ remain in $B_h$ for all $t\geq t_0$. If it never enters $K_h$ after $t_0$, then <a href="#eq:trajectory-increase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trajectory-increase">[eq:trajectory-increase]</a> gives $$F_h(x(t))\geq F_h(x(t_0))+\delta_h(t-t_0)
 \qquad(t\geq t_0),$$ contradicting $F_h\leq M_h$ for sufficiently large $t$. It must enter $K_h$ at a finite time, and the forward invariance just proved keeps it there. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:permanence"></a>
### A common absorbing set for each class

<a id="eq:scale-containment"></a>
Fix a positive stoichiometric class $P$ and its representative $c$, and retain the scale $h_P$ and the functions $F_h$ from Section <a href="#sec:trapping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:trapping">3</a>. Lemma <a href="#lem:trapping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:trapping">6</a> gives invariance at each scale and finite-time entry whenever a trajectory stays in the corresponding box. Proposition <a href="#prop:plateau" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:plateau">4</a> connects these two properties through $$\label{eq:scale-containment}
 K_h\subset\operatorname{int}B_{\sqrt h}
 \qquad(0<h\leq h_P).$$ We now use this relation to replace an initial-point-dependent trapping set by one fixed set for $P$.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-trapping-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-trapping</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Let $x^0\in P$. Since $x^0$ is positive, we may choose $0<h_0\leq h_P$ so small that $$\left\|\frac{\log x^0}{\log h_0}\right\|_\infty<H,$$ where the logarithm is componentwise and $H>0$ is the fixed separation constant from Section <a href="#sec:trapping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:trapping">3</a>. In particular, $x^0\in B_{h_0}$. The coordinate sign comparisons force every label active at $x^0$ to have slope zero, just as they did at $c$. An active constant label must have the least value among all constant labels, namely $M_{h_0}=F_{h_0}(c)$. Hence $$x^0\in K_{h_0}.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:trapping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:trapping">6</a> implies that the solution through $x^0$ is global, stays positive, and remains in $K_{h_0}$. Since $f(x)\in S$, it also remains in $P$.

<a id="eq:scale-sequence"></a>
Starting at $h_0$, define $$\label{eq:scale-sequence}
 h_{j+1}=\min\{\sqrt{h_j},h_P\}.$$ We claim that for every $j$ there is a finite time $T_j$ such that $x(t)\in K_{h_j}$ for all $t\geq T_j$. The case $j=0$ holds with $T_0=0$. Suppose it holds at $j$. Because $h_{j+1}\leq\sqrt{h_j}$, the boxes satisfy $B_{\sqrt{h_j}}\subset B_{h_{j+1}}$. Thus <a href="#eq:scale-containment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scale-containment">[eq:scale-containment]</a> yields $$x(t)\in B_{h_{j+1}}\quad(t\geq T_j).$$ The finite-entry assertion of Lemma <a href="#lem:trapping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:trapping">6</a>, followed by its invariance assertion, supplies a finite $T_{j+1}\geq T_j$ with $x(t)\in K_{h_{j+1}}$ for all $t\geq T_{j+1}$. This proves the claim. The argument uses containment in successive boxes; no nesting of the sets $K_h$ is assumed.

Repeated square roots of $h_0\in(0,1)$ tend to $1$. Since $h_P<1$, the capped sequence <a href="#eq:scale-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scale-sequence">[eq:scale-sequence]</a> therefore reaches $h_P$ after finitely many steps. We conclude that every solution through a point of $P$ enters $K_{h_P}$ in finite time and stays there.

Finally set $$K_P=K_{h_P}\cap(c+S).$$ This is nonempty because it contains $c$, and it is compact and convex as the intersection of the compact convex set $K_{h_P}$ with a closed affine subspace. Since $K_{h_P}$ lies in the positive orthant, $K_P\subset P$. Both $K_{h_P}$ and the affine subspace $c+S$ are forward invariant, so $K_P$ is forward invariant. The preceding argument proves its absorbing property. Moreover, $K_P\subset B_{h_P}$, and therefore $\varepsilon_P=h_P\in(0,1)$ gives $$\varepsilon_P\leq x_i(t)\leq\varepsilon_P^{-1}
 \quad(1\leq i\leq d)$$ for all sufficiently large $t$, with the entry time allowed to depend on $x^0$. The scale $h_P$, and hence these bounds, was fixed before choosing the initial point. ◻

</details>
